Um, well... I guess maybe a little? But I think a lot of people do that, right? Like, just wanting to stay healthy? 

It's just... if I eat something heavy, like, um, the fried food someone leaves in the break room at work, or if there's a big plate of food at church... Um, I don't know if I'd use the word controlling? That sounds a little extreme, and I'm really trying my best to manage everything well. But... well, I guess it does take up a lot of space in my head sometimes. 

Like, if I were to eat something heavy... like the fried food they sometimes leave in the break room at the packing plant, or... or if I had one of those big plates of food at church. I just get so scared that it’s going to show on my body right away, you know? Like instantly. But... I mean, it's pretty normal for women to care about their figure and want to weigh themselves, isn't it? I'm just trying to be careful and stay on top of things. I hope that makes sense? I'm really trying to give you the right answers here.

Um, I wouldn't say I'm avoiding social activities completely, no. I'm definitely still going to church every Sunday, and I talk to people at work during my shifts. I really try to be friendly and present, I promise!

But I still see people! I'm not, like, hiding away in my room or anything. I just prefer to socialize in ways that don't involve food, if that makes sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound antisocial or anything... I just like to be careful. Is that okay?

Um, distress? I mean... I wouldn't call it distress, really. That sounds like I'm falling apart or something, and I promise I'm holding it together pretty well!

Um, well, I wouldn't say I've stopped doing the important things! I'm definitely still showing up for all my shifts at the plant, and I go to every single one of my GED classes. I really pride myself on being responsible with all of that, I promise.

I really hope you don't think I'm doing something wrong.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I have lost some weight, yeah. I mean, my work pants are definitely a lot looser than they used to be, and like I mentioned, people at church have been saying I look a little too thin lately. \n\nBut... I don't know, when I step on the scale and see the number go down, it just feels like... like I'm finally getting somewhere? Like I'm actually doing a good job at something. I just don't really see why everyone thinks I need to put the weight back on. I'm just trying to be disciplined, you know? That's a good thing, right? I hope you don't think I'm being difficult or anything... I just really feel better this way.\n\nDoctor: Are there any parts of your daily routine or activities that you've had to change or avoid because of your eating habits or fear of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um, I think I kind of mentioned this a little bit earlier? But... yeah, I guess there are a few things. I'm definitely still going to all my packing shifts and showing up for my classes, I promise! I'm really trying to be responsible and not let anyone down. \n\nBut... like I said before, I have been turning down those meals after church. It's just... I feel like I have to keep my eating and my schedule really controlled, and going out with everyone just messes all that up. And... I feel really bad admitting this, but that's also why I've been leaving some of my GED homework unfinished lately. I just get so focused on planning out my food and my routines that I sort of run out of time or get too distracted to finish the assignments. \n\nBut I'm still showing up for the actual classes! That's the most important part, right? I'm really trying my best to keep it all together... I hope I'm answering your questions okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your fear of gaining weight is controlling your life?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't know if I'd use the word controlling? That sounds a little extreme, and I'm really trying my best to manage everything well. But... well, I guess it does take up a lot of space in my head sometimes. \n\nLike, if I were to eat something heavy... like the fried food they sometimes leave in the break room at the packing plant, or... or if I had one of those big plates of food at church. I just get so scared that it’s going to show on my body right away, you know? Like instantly. So if that happens, I'll just keep thinking about it all day, and I get this really strong urge to go check the scale just to make sure I didn't ruin anything. \n\nBut... I mean, it's pretty normal for women to care about their figure and want to weigh themselves, isn't it? I'm just trying to be careful and stay on top of things. I hope that makes sense? I'm really trying to give you the right answers here.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding social activities because of your fear of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um, I wouldn't say I'm avoiding social activities completely, no. I don't know, when I step on the scale and see the number go down, it just feels like... like I'm finally getting somewhere? Like I'm actually doing a good job at something. I just don't really see why everyone thinks I need to put the weight back on. I'm just trying to be disciplined, you know? That's a good thing, right? I hope you don't think I'm being difficult or anything... I just really feel better this way.\n\nDoctor: Are there any parts of your daily routine or activities that you've had to change or avoid because of your eating habits or fear of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um, I think I kind of mentioned this a little bit earlier? I'm really trying to be responsible and not let anyone down. \n\nBut... like I said before, I have been turning down those meals after church. It's just... I just get so focused on planning out my food and my routines that I sort of run out of time or get too distracted to finish the assignments. \n\nBut I'm still showing up for the actual classes! That's the most important part, right? I'm really trying my best to keep it all together... I hope I'm answering your questions okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your fear of gaining weight is controlling your life?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't know if I'd use the word controlling? That sounds a little extreme, and I'm really trying my best to manage everything well. But... well, I guess it does take up a lot of space in my head sometimes. \n\nLike, if I were to eat something heavy... like the fried food they sometimes leave in the break room at the packing plant, or... or if I had one of those big plates of food at church. I just get so scared that it’s going to show on my body right away, you know? Like instantly. I wouldn't call it distress, really. But I wouldn't say it's causing me, like, major distress. I hope I'm not coming across like I'm a mess or anything... I'm doing okay, really. Does that answer what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy but no longer participate in because of your concerns about eating or gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I wouldn't say I've stopped doing the important things! Um... affecting my relationships? I... I don't think so. I mean, not really. I'm still really close with my family, and I still see people at work and church.

But... yeah, I guess there are a few things. I'm definitely still going to all my packing shifts and showing up for my classes, I promise! I'm really trying to be responsible and not let anyone down. \n\nBut... like I said before, I have been turning down those meals after church. It's just... I feel like I have to keep my eating and my schedule really controlled, and going out with everyone just messes all that up. And... I feel really bad admitting this, but that's also why I've been leaving some of my GED homework unfinished lately. I just get so focused on planning out my food and my routines that I sort of run out of time or get too distracted to finish the assignments. \n\nBut I'm still showing up for the actual classes! That's the most important part, right? I'm really trying my best to keep it all together... I hope I'm answering your questions okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your fear of gaining weight is controlling your life?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't know if I'd use the word controlling? That sounds a little extreme, and I'm really trying my best to manage everything well. But... well, I guess it does take up a lot of space in my head sometimes. \n\nLike, if I were to eat something heavy... like the fried food they sometimes leave in the break room at the packing plant, or... or if I had one of those big plates of food at church. I just get so scared that it’s going to show on my body right away, you know? Like instantly. So if that happens, I'll just keep thinking about it all day, and I get this really strong urge to go check the scale just to make sure I didn't ruin anything. \n\nBut... I mean, it's pretty normal for women to care about their figure and want to weigh themselves, isn't it? I'm just trying to be careful and stay on top of things. I hope that makes sense? I'm really trying to give you the right answers here.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding social activities because of your fear of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um, I wouldn't say I'm avoiding social activities completely, no. I'm definitely still going to church every Sunday, and I talk to people at work during my shifts. I really try to be friendly and present, I promise! \n\nIt's just... well, like I mentioned earlier, I do skip the meals after church now. And... I guess if someone at work asks if I want to grab a bite after our shift, I usually make up an excuse to go home instead. It just feels safer to eat what I've prepared for myself. Going out to eat with people just feels really overwhelming right now, because I don't know exactly how things are cooked or what's in them. \n\nBut I still see people! I'm not, like, hiding away in my room or anything. I just prefer to socialize in ways that don't involve food, if that makes sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound antisocial or anything... I just like to be careful. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your fear of gaining weight is causing you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Um, distress? I mean... I wouldn't call it distress, really. That sounds like I'm falling apart or something, and I promise I'm holding it together pretty well! \n\nI guess... sometimes it gets a little stressful, just trying to keep track of everything and making sure I don't mess up my routine. Like when I'm trying to do my GED homework and my mind just keeps drifting back to what I'm going to eat the next day, or if I need to adjust my calories... it can be a little frustrating. I think everyone gets a little stressed out by their routines sometimes, right? \n\nI'm really trying to just stay focused and do the right things. I hope I'm not coming across like I'm a mess or anything... I'm doing okay, really. I guess, like I mentioned, I have been turning down the meals after church, which I used to really look forward to. I get so focused on making sure my eating and my schedule are completely controlled for the next day, and planning all that out takes up a lot of the evening when I'd usually be studying. \n\nI'm still going to class, though! I just really need to make sure my routine is perfectly set so I don't mess anything up. I hope you don't think I'm slacking off... I'm really trying my absolute best to balance everything. I guess I sort of just mentioned this, but... I really wouldn't say it's a huge impact. I mean, I'm still doing all the important stuff! I haven't missed a single shift at the packaging plant, and I'm still sitting in my seat for every GED class. I'm really dedicated to being reliable, I promise.\n\nBut... um, if I had to pick something, I guess it would just be what I was saying about church and school. I really miss staying for the meals after service, but it's just so much easier to eat at home where I know exactly what's happening. And... yeah, the homework thing. I feel really bad about leaving some of it unfinished lately. It's just... by the time I get home, I get so wrapped up in planning out exactly what I'm going to eat the next day and making sure my schedule is perfectly set so I don't gain anything... the time just slips away from me. \n\nI don't want you to think I'm letting my life fall apart, though! I'm still working hard, and I'm still trying. It's just a little hard to juggle the homework with making sure I stay on track with my eating. I hope that makes sense? I'm really trying not to let it get out of hand, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your eating habits are interfering with your ability to complete your GED?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know, when I step on the scale and see the number go down, it just feels like... like I'm finally getting somewhere? Like I'm actually doing a good job at something. I hope you don't think I'm being difficult or anything... I just really feel better this way.\n\nDoctor: Are there any parts of your daily routine or activities that you've had to change or avoid because of your eating habits or fear of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um, I think I kind of mentioned this a little bit earlier? But... yeah, I guess there are a few things. I'm definitely still going to all my packing shifts and showing up for my classes, I promise! I'm really trying to be responsible and not let anyone down. \n\nBut... like I said before, I have been turning down those meals after church. It's just... I feel like I have to keep my eating and my schedule really controlled, and going out with everyone just messes all that up. And... I feel really bad admitting this, but that's also why I've been leaving some of my GED homework unfinished lately. I just get so focused on planning out my food and my routines that I sort of run out of time or get too distracted to finish the assignments. \n\nBut I'm still showing up for the actual classes! That's the most important part, right? I'm really trying my best to keep it all together... I hope I'm answering your questions okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your fear of gaining weight is controlling your life?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't know if I'd use the word controlling? That sounds a little extreme, and I'm really trying my best to manage everything well. But... well, I guess it does take up a lot of space in my head sometimes. \n\nLike, if I were to eat something heavy... like the fried food they sometimes leave in the break room at the packing plant, or... or if I had one of those big plates of food at church. I just get so scared that it’s going to show on my body right away, you know? Like instantly. So if that happens, I'll just keep thinking about it all day, and I get this really strong urge to go check the scale just to make sure I didn't ruin anything. \n\nBut... I mean, it's pretty normal for women to care about their figure and want to weigh themselves, isn't it? I'm just trying to be careful and stay on top of things. I hope that makes sense? I'm really trying to give you the right answers here.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding social activities because of your fear of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um, I wouldn't say I'm avoiding social activities completely, no. I'm definitely still going to church every Sunday, and I talk to people at work during my shifts. I really try to be friendly and present, I promise! \n\nIt's just... well, like I mentioned earlier, I do skip the meals after church now. And... I guess if someone at work asks if I want to grab a bite after our shift, I usually make up an excuse to go home instead. It just feels safer to eat what I've prepared for myself. Going out to eat with people just feels really overwhelming right now, because I don't know exactly how things are cooked or what's in them. \n\nBut I still see people! I just like to be careful. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your fear of gaining weight is causing you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Um, distress? I mean... I wouldn't call it distress, really. But I wouldn't say it's causing me, like, major distress. I think everyone gets a little stressed out by their routines sometimes, right? \n\nI'm really trying to just stay focused and do the right things. I hope I'm not coming across like I'm a mess or anything... I'm doing okay, really. Does that answer what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy but no longer participate in because of your concerns about eating or gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I wouldn't say I've stopped doing the important things! I guess, like I mentioned, I have been turning down the meals after church, which I used to really look forward to. I haven't missed a single shift at the packaging plant, and I'm still sitting in my seat for every GED class. And, um... sometimes I do leave my GED homework unfinished. I don't mean to be a bad student! It's just... I get so focused on making sure my eating and my schedule are perfectly controlled, and it takes up so much of my mental energy. By the time I sit down to do the homework, I just... People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... happier if I weighed more? Oh, no, I... I don't think so. I mean, I know I've lost some weight, and my work pants are definitely a bit loose now. And, well, some people have mentioned that I look a little thin... but they're just being nice and looking out for me, I'm sure. 

But honestly, when I step on the scale and see the number go down, it just... it feels like I'm finally accomplishing something, you know? Like I'm actually getting somewhere with all the discipline and effort I'm putting in. It makes me feel like I'm doing a good job. 

I just... I don't really understand why I should be pushed to put the weight back on. I mean, I'm still healthy and working hard, right? It's not a bad thing to be a little smaller, is it? I really don't want to disappoint anyone, but the thought of gaining it back just... it doesn't feel right to me. That's okay to feel that way, isn't it?

But... well, I guess it does take up a lot of space in my head sometimes. \n\nLike, if I were to eat something heavy... like the fried food they sometimes leave in the break room at the packing plant, or... or if I had one of those big plates of food at church. I just get so scared that it’s going to show on my body right away, you know? Like instantly. So if that happens, I'll just keep thinking about it all day, and I get this really strong urge to go check the scale just to make sure I didn't ruin anything. \n\nBut... I mean, it's pretty normal for women to care about their figure and want to weigh themselves, isn't it? I'm just trying to be careful and stay on top of things. I hope that makes sense? I'm really trying to give you the right answers here.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding social activities because of your fear of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um, I wouldn't say I'm avoiding social activities completely, no. I'm definitely still going to church every Sunday, and I talk to people at work during my shifts. I really try to be friendly and present, I promise! \n\nIt's just... well, like I mentioned earlier, I do skip the meals after church now. And... I guess if someone at work asks if I want to grab a bite after our shift, I usually make up an excuse to go home instead. It just feels safer to eat what I've prepared for myself. Going out to eat with people just feels really overwhelming right now, because I don't know exactly how things are cooked or what's in them. \n\nBut I still see people! I'm not, like, hiding away in my room or anything. I just prefer to socialize in ways that don't involve food, if that makes sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound antisocial or anything... I just like to be careful. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your fear of gaining weight is causing you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Um, distress? I mean... I guess, like I mentioned, I have been turning down the meals after church, which I used to really look forward to. I hope that doesn't sound too crazy to you.\n\nDoctor: Does worrying about gaining weight affect your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Um, I wouldn't say it affects things too much? Like, I'm still showing up for all my packing shifts and making it to my GED classes, so I'm keeping up with my responsibilities. I promise I'm not letting things fall apart. \n\nI guess... well, maybe a little bit? Like, after church, sometimes people want to go grab a meal together, and I've been saying no to that lately. It's just easier to avoid the stress of figuring out what to eat in front of everyone. And... I guess sometimes I get a little behind on my GED homework because I'm just so focused on planning my schedule and making sure my eating stays controlled. \n\nBut I'm still functioning fine overall! It's just a few little things here and there. That's okay, right? I mean, everyone gets a little distracted sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is currently at a healthy level?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I mean, I feel like I'm okay? Like, I know my regular doctor mentioned something about it being a little low, and... and some of the ladies at church have been asking if I'm eating enough, which is sweet of them, but... I honestly think they're all just making a bigger deal out of it than it really is. \n\nLike I said, I'm still going to my packing shifts every day, I'm still doing my GED classes, and I'm keeping up with my life, you know? If my weight was actually at a dangerous level, I wouldn't be able to do all that, right? I'd be sick in bed or something. So I think I'm doing just fine. It's perfectly healthy to be on the lighter side, isn't it? I just... I really hope you don't think I'm doing something wrong.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I have lost some weight, yeah. I mean, my work pants are definitely a lot looser than they used to be, and like I mentioned, people at church have been saying I look a little too thin lately. \n\nBut... I don't know, when I step on the scale and see the number go down, it just feels like... like I'm finally getting somewhere? Like I'm actually doing a good job at something. I just don't really see why everyone thinks I need to put the weight back on. I'm just trying to be disciplined, you know? That's a good thing, right? I hope you don't think I'm being difficult or anything... I just really feel better this way.\n\nDoctor: Are there any parts of your daily routine or activities that you've had to change or avoid because of your eating habits or fear of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um, I think I kind of mentioned this a little bit earlier? But... yeah, I guess there are a few things. I'm definitely still going to all my packing shifts and showing up for my classes, I promise! I'm really trying to be responsible and not let anyone down. \n\nBut... like I said before, I have been turning down those meals after church. It's just... I feel like I have to keep my eating and my schedule really controlled, and going out with everyone just messes all that up. And... I feel really bad admitting this, but that's also why I've been leaving some of my GED homework unfinished lately. I just get so focused on planning out my food and my routines that I sort of run out of time or get too distracted to finish the assignments. \n\nBut I'm still showing up for the actual classes! That's the most important part, right? I'm really trying my best to keep it all together... I hope I'm answering your questions okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your fear of gaining weight is controlling your life?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't know if I'd use the word controlling? That sounds a little extreme, and I'm really trying my best to manage everything well. But... well, I guess it does take up a lot of space in my head sometimes. \n\nLike, if I were to eat something heavy... like the fried food they sometimes leave in the break room at the packing plant, or... or if I had one of those big plates of food at church. I just get so scared that it’s going to show on my body right away, you know? Like instantly. So if that happens, I'll just keep thinking about it all day, and I get this really strong urge to go check the scale just to make sure I didn't ruin anything. \n\nBut... I mean, it's pretty normal for women to care about their figure and want to weigh themselves, isn't it? I'm just trying to be careful and stay on top of things. I hope that makes sense? I'm really trying to give you the right answers here.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding social activities because of your fear of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um, I wouldn't say I'm avoiding social activities completely, no. I'm definitely still going to church every Sunday, and I talk to people at work during my shifts. I really try to be friendly and present, I promise! \n\nIt's just... well, like I mentioned earlier, I do skip the meals after church now. And... I guess if someone at work asks if I want to grab a bite after our shift, I usually make up an excuse to go home instead. It just feels safer to eat what I've prepared for myself. Going out to eat with people just feels really overwhelming right now, because I don't know exactly how things are cooked or what's in them. \n\nBut I still see people! I'm not, like, hiding away in my room or anything. I just prefer to socialize in ways that don't involve food, if that makes sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound antisocial or anything... I just like to be careful. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your fear of gaining weight is causing you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Um, distress? I mean... I wouldn't call it distress, really. That sounds like I'm falling apart or something, and I promise I'm holding it together pretty well! \n\nI guess... sometimes it gets a little stressful, just trying to keep track of everything and making sure I don't mess up my routine. Like when I'm trying to do my GED homework and my mind just keeps drifting back to what I'm going to eat the next day, or if I need to adjust my calories... it can be a little frustrating. But I wouldn't say it's causing me, like, major distress. I think everyone gets a little stressed out by their routines sometimes, right? \n\nI'm really trying to just stay focused and do the right things. I hope I'm not coming across like I'm a mess or anything... I'm doing okay, really. Does that answer what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy but no longer participate in because of your concerns about eating or gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I wouldn't say I've stopped doing the important things! I'm definitely still showing up for all my shifts at the plant, and I go to every single one of my GED classes. I really pride myself on being responsible with all of that, I promise. \n\nBut... I guess, like I mentioned, I have been turning down the meals after church, which I used to really look forward to. And... um, I feel a little embarrassed to admit this to you, but lately I’ve been leaving some of my GED homework unfinished. It's not that I'm being lazy or anything! It's just... I get so focused on making sure my eating and my schedule are completely controlled for the next day, and planning all that out takes up a lot of the evening when I'd usually be studying. \n\nI'm still going to class, though! I just really need to make sure my routine is perfectly set so I don't mess anything up. I hope you don't think I'm slacking off... I'm really trying my absolute best to balance everything. Is it okay that I'm prioritizing keeping my schedule organized right now?\n\nDoctor: Are there any aspects of your life where you feel like your eating habits or fear of gaining weight are having a more significant impact than you'd like?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I sort of just mentioned this, but... I really wouldn't say it's a huge impact. I mean, I'm still doing all the important stuff! I haven't missed a single shift at the packaging plant, and I'm still sitting in my seat for every GED class. I'm really dedicated to being reliable, I promise.\n\nBut... um, if I had to pick something, I guess it would just be what I was saying about church and school. I really miss staying for the meals after service, but it's just so much easier to eat at home where I know exactly what's happening. And... yeah, the homework thing. I feel really bad about leaving some of it unfinished lately. It's just... by the time I get home, I get so wrapped up in planning out exactly what I'm going to eat the next day and making sure my schedule is perfectly set so I don't gain anything... the time just slips away from me. \n\nI don't want you to think I'm letting my life fall apart, though! I'm still working hard, and I'm still trying. It's just a little hard to juggle the homework with making sure I stay on track with my eating. I hope that makes sense? I'm really trying not to let it get out of hand, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your eating habits are interfering with your ability to complete your GED?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, \"interfering\" sounds so... so strong, you know? Like I'm failing or something. And I'm definitely not failing! I'm still going to every single class, sitting right there in the front. I haven't missed a day. \n\nIt's just... well, like I was saying before, it's just the homework part that's gotten a little tricky lately. I sit down to do it, but then my mind just starts racing about my meal plan for tomorrow, and I have to get my schedule completely figured out so I feel safe and in control. And before I know it, it's late, and some of the assignments just... don't get done. \n\nBut I'm really not trying to be a bad student! I'm still working my shifts at the plant, and I'm still trying my best. It's just... between making sure my food is perfectly planned and, you know, skipping those church meals so I don't mess up my routine... the homework just sort of falls through the cracks sometimes. \n\nI'm still a good student, though, right? I mean, showing up to class has to count for something. I really hope you don't think I'm just giving up on my GED.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit or use laxatives after eating?\n\nPatient: Oh, no! No, no, I would never do anything like that. Um, I know some people do that, but I promise I don't. I've never made myself sick or taken laxatives or anything. \n\nI just... I just try to be really careful and disciplined about what I eat in the first place, so I don't ever feel like I need to do those things. That's why I spend so much time planning my meals and my schedule, like I was saying. I just want to do things the right way, you know? \n\nI hope you believe me. I wouldn't lie to you about something like that. I'm really just trying to be healthy and careful. That's a good thing, right? Being disciplined?\n\nDoctor: Do you think about your weight or eating habits for more than an hour every day?\n\nPatient: Um... more than an hour? I guess... I mean, yes, probably. Especially if I'm worried about making a mistake. I just know I'd get so scared that it was going to show on me right away. \n\nSo I guess I do spend a lot of time thinking about it, just to make sure I don't put myself in those situations. And if I do feel like I messed up, I... I kind of can't stop thinking about it, and I'll want to go check the scale just to be sure I'm still okay. \n\nBut I'm just trying to be careful, I promise. It's not like I'm obsessing over it in a bad way... I just want to stay on track. That's okay, isn't it? Being mindful about what you eat?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your fear of gaining weight is affecting your relationships with friends and family?\n\nPatient: Um... affecting my relationships? I... I don't think so. I mean, not really. I'm still really close with my family, and I still see people at work and church. \n\nI guess... well, I did mention that I skip the meals after the service now. Trying to balance work and school and... and everything else? I'm really trying not to let it get out of hand, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your eating habits are interfering with your ability to complete your GED?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, \"interfering\" sounds so... so strong, you know? Like I'm failing or something. And I'm definitely not failing! I'm still going to every single class, sitting right there in the front. I haven't missed a day. \n\nIt's just... well, like I was saying before, it's just the homework part that's gotten a little tricky lately. I sit down to do it, but then my mind just starts racing about my meal plan for tomorrow, and I have to get my schedule completely figured out so I feel safe and in control. And before I know it, it's late, and some of the assignments just... don't get done. \n\nBut I'm really not trying to be a bad student! I'm still working my shifts at the plant, and I'm still trying my best. It's just... between making sure my food is perfectly planned and, you know, skipping those church meals so I don't mess up my routine... the homework just sort of falls through the cracks sometimes. \n\nI'm still a good student, though, right? I mean, showing up to class has to count for something. I really hope you don't think I'm just giving up on my GED.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit or use laxatives after eating?\n\nPatient: Oh, no! No, no, I would never do anything like that. Um, I know some people do that, but I promise I don't. I've never made myself sick or taken laxatives or anything. \n\nI just... I just try to be really careful and disciplined about what I eat in the first place, so I don't ever feel like I need to do those things. That's why I spend so much time planning my meals and my schedule, like I was saying. I just want to do things the right way, you know? \n\nI hope you believe me. I wouldn't lie to you about something like that. I'm really just trying to be healthy and careful. That's a good thing, right? Being disciplined?\n\nDoctor: Do you think about your weight or eating habits for more than an hour every day?\n\nPatient: Um... more than an hour? I guess... I mean, yes, probably. But isn't it normal to think about what you're eating? I just like to be prepared, you know? \n\nBut, um... if I'm being completely honest... sometimes it does take up a bit more time. Especially if I'm worried about making a mistake. Like, if I were to eat something bad... um, like if someone brought fried food into the break room at the plant, or if I actually stayed for one of those big plates at church... I just know I'd get so scared that it was going to show on me right away. \n\nSo I guess I do spend a lot of time thinking about it, just to make sure I don't put myself in those situations. And if I do feel like I messed up, I... I kind of can't stop thinking about it, and I'll want to go check the scale just to be sure I'm still okay. \n\nBut I'm just trying to be careful, I promise. It's not like I'm obsessing over it in a bad way... I just want to stay on track. That's okay, isn't it? Being mindful about what you eat?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your fear of gaining weight is affecting your relationships with friends and family?\n\nPatient: Um... affecting my relationships? I... I don't think so. I mean, not really. I'm still really close with my family, and I still see people at work and church. \n\nI guess... well, I did mention that I skip the meals after the service now. And sometimes my mom gets a little... um, a little worried when I don't eat what she cooks. She might say something, and I just tell her I'm not hungry or I already ate at work. I just don't want her to worry, you know? It's easier that way.\n\nBut I wouldn't say it's *affecting* our relationship in a bad way! We still talk all the time. I just... I just have to be a little careful about social things if there's food involved. It's just easier to avoid those situations so I don't have to explain myself or make anyone uncomfortable. \n\nThat makes sense, right? It's pretty normal to just want to avoid awkward situations with family, isn't it? I really try my best to be a good daughter and a good friend, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your eating habits and fear of gaining weight are controlling your life most of the time?\n\nPatient: Um... controlling my life? It takes a lot of energy to keep everything on track, you know? \n\nBut I'm still functioning perfectly fine! I just have a really structured routine right now. It's good to have structure and be disciplined, right? I'm just trying to make sure I do everything correctly. You don't think that means it's controlling me, do you?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is significantly interfering with your ability to enjoy or participate in daily activities?\n\nPatient: Um... interfering with my daily activities? Well, like I was saying before, I really do try my hardest to keep up with everything. I'm still showing up for all my shifts at the packing plant, and I haven't missed a GED class. I really pride myself on being reliable. \n\nBut... I guess if you mean enjoying things... well, I did say I've been turning down the meals after church. Trying to balance work and school and... and everything else? I'm really trying my best not to let it get in the way too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be happier with your body if you weighed a little more?\n\nPatient: Um... happier if I weighed more? Oh, no, I... I don't think so. I mean, I know I've lost some weight, and my work pants are definitely a bit loose now. And, well, some people have mentioned that I look a little thin... but they're just being nice and looking out for me, I'm sure. \n\nBut honestly, when I step on the scale and see the number go down, it just... it feels like I'm finally accomplishing something, you know? Like I'm actually getting somewhere with all the discipline and effort I'm putting in. It makes me feel like I'm doing a good job. \n\nI just...
